ReGA: Model-Based Safeguard for LLMs via Representation-Guided Abstraction
Authors: Zeming Wei, Chengcan Wu, Meng Sun
First: 2025-06-02T15:17:38+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-18T05:40:12+00:00
Comments: FSE 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large Language Models (LLMs) have achieved tremendous success in various tasks, yet concerns about their safety and security have emerged. In particular, they pose risks of generating harmful content and are vulnerable to jailbreaking attacks, creating unaddressed security issues regarding their deployments. In the context of software engineering for artificial intelligence (SE4AI) techniques, model-based analysis has demonstrated notable potential for analyzing and monitoring machine learning models, particularly in stateful deep neural networks. However, it suffers from scalability issues when extended to LLMs due to their vast feature spaces. In this paper, we aim to address the scalability issue of model-based analysis techniques for safeguarding LLM-scale models. Motivated by the recent discovery of low-dimensional safety-critical representations that emerged in LLMs, we propose ReGA, a model-based analysis framework with Representation-Guided Abstraction, to safeguard LLMs against harmful prompts and generations. By leveraging safety-critical representations, which are key directions in hidden states that indicate safety-related concepts, ReGA effectively narrows the scalability gap when developing the abstract model for safety modeling. Our comprehensive evaluation shows that ReGA performs sufficiently well in distinguishing between safe and harmful inputs, achieving an AUROC of 0.975 at the prompt level and 0.985 at the conversation level. Additionally, ReGA exhibits robustness to real-world attacks and generalization across different safety perspectives, outperforming existing safeguard paradigms in terms of interpretability and scalability. Overall, ReGA serves as an efficient and scalable solution to enhance LLM safety by integrating representation engineering with model-based abstraction, paving the way for new paradigms to utilize software insights for AI safety.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ReGA：基于模型的LLM安全保障通过表示引导抽象
大型语言模型（LLMs）在各种任务中取得了巨大的成功，但关于其安全性和保障性的问题也随之而来。特别是，它们存在生成有害内容的风险，并且容易受到越狱攻击，导致其部署存在未解决的安全问题。在人工智能软件工程（SE4AI）技术的背景下，基于模型的分析在分析和监控机器学习模型方面显示出显著潜力，特别是在有状态的深度神经网络中。然而，当扩展到LLMs时，由于其庞大的特征空间，它面临可扩展性问题。本文旨在解决基于模型的分析技术在保护LLM规模模型时的可扩展性问题。受到最近发现的在LLMs中出现的低维安全关键表示的启发，我们提出了ReGA，一个基于模型的分析框架，采用表示引导抽象，以保护LLMs免受有害提示和生成的影响。通过利用安全关键表示，这些表示是指示安全相关概念的隐藏状态中的关键方向，ReGA有效缩小了在安全建模中开发抽象模型时的可扩展性差距。我们的综合评估表明，ReGA在区分安全和有害输入方面表现良好，在提示级别上实现了0.975的AUROC，在对话级别上实现了0.985。此外，ReGA对现实世界攻击表现出鲁棒性，并在不同安全视角下具有良好的泛化能力，在可解释性和可扩展性方面超越了现有的安全保障范式。总体而言，ReGA作为一种高效且可扩展的解决方案，通过将表示工程与基于模型的抽象相结合，增强了LLM的安全性，为利用软件洞察力提升AI安全性开辟了新范式。

Previous methods in model-based analysis for safeguarding machine learning models have faced scalability challenges when applied to LLMs due to their extensive feature spaces. SafeDream: Safety World Model for Proactive Early Jailbreak Detection
Authors: Bo Yan, Weikai Lin, Yada Zhu, Song Wang
First: 2026-04-18T04:31:33+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-18T04:31:33+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Multi-turn jailbreak attacks progressively erode LLM safety alignment across seemingly innocuous conversation turns, achieving success rates exceeding 90% against state-of-the-art models. Existing alignment-based and guardrail methods suffer from three key limitations: they require costly weight modification, evaluate each turn independently without modeling cumulative safety erosion, and detect attacks only after harmful content has been generated. To address these limitations, we first formulate the proactive early jailbreak detection problem with a new metric, detection lead, that measures how early an attack can be detected before the LLM complies. We then propose SAFEDREAM, a lightweight world-model-based framework that operates as an external module without modifying the LLM's weights. SAFEDREAM introduces three components: (1) a safety state world model that encodes LLM hidden states into a compact safety representation and predicts how it evolves across turns, (2) CUSUM detection that accumulates weak per-turn risk signals into reliable evidence, and (3) contrastive imagination that simultaneously rolls out attack and benign futures in latent space to issue early alarms before jailbreaks occur. On three multi-turn jailbreak benchmarks (XGuard-Train, SafeDialBench, SafeMTData) against 8 baselines, SAFEDREAM achieves the best detection timeliness across all benchmarks (1.06-1.20 turns before compliance) while maintaining competitive false positive rates and outperforming baselines in detection quality.
中文标题/摘要
标题：SafeDream：主动早期越狱检测的安全世界模型
多轮越狱攻击逐渐侵蚀大型语言模型（LLM）的安全对齐，在看似无害的对话轮次中成功率超过90%，针对最先进的模型。现有的基于对齐和护栏的方法存在三个主要局限性：需要昂贵的权重修改，独立评估每个轮次而不建模累积的安全侵蚀，并且仅在生成有害内容后检测攻击。为了解决这些局限性，我们首先用一个新的指标——检测提前量，来制定主动早期越狱检测问题，该指标衡量攻击在LLM遵从之前可以多早被检测到。然后，我们提出了SAFEDREAM，一个轻量级的基于世界模型的框架，作为外部模块运行而不修改LLM的权重。SAFEDREAM引入了三个组件：（1）安全状态世界模型，将LLM的隐藏状态编码为紧凑的安全表示，并预测其在轮次中的演变；（2）CUSUM检测，将每轮的微弱风险信号累积为可靠证据；（3）对比想象，同时在潜在空间中展开攻击和良性未来，以在越狱发生之前发出早期警报。在三个多轮越狱基准（XGuard-Train、SafeDialBench、SafeMTData）上与8个基线进行比较，SAFEDREAM在所有基准中实现了最佳检测及时性（在遵从之前1.06-1.20轮），同时保持竞争性的误报率，并在检测质量上优于基线。

SafeLM: Unified Privacy-Aware Optimization for Trustworthy Federated Large Language Models
Authors: Noor Islam S. Mohammad, Uluğ Bayazıt
First: 2026-04-17T18:00:58+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-17T18:00:58+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in high-stakes domains, yet a unified treatment of their overlapping safety challenges remains lacking. We present SafeLM, a framework that jointly addresses four pillars of LLM safety: privacy, security, misinformation, and adversarial robustness. SafeLM combines federated training with gradient smartification and Paillier encryption for privacy, integrates defenses against training and inference-time attacks, employs contrastive grounding with calibrated decoding to reduce hallucinations, and introduces alignment-aware binarized aggregation to enhance robustness while maintaining bounded reconstruction quality. Across benchmarks on factuality, toxicity, and membership inference, SafeLM achieves 98.0% harmful content detection accuracy, reduces communication by 96.9%, and lowers gradient inversion PSNR from 31.7 dB to 15.1 dB. Ablations show that each component contributes independently, whereas their integration yields a strong privacy utility efficiency trade-off for deploying trustworthy LLMs.
中文标题/摘要
标题：SafeLM：可信联邦大型语言模型的统一隐私意识优化
大型语言模型（LLMs）越来越多地应用于高风险领域，但对其重叠安全挑战的统一处理仍然缺乏。我们提出了SafeLM，一个共同解决LLM安全四大支柱的框架：隐私、安全、错误信息和对抗鲁棒性。SafeLM结合了联邦训练、梯度智能化和Paillier加密以保护隐私，整合了针对训练和推理时攻击的防御，采用对比基础与校准解码以减少幻觉，并引入对齐感知的二值聚合以增强鲁棒性，同时保持有限的重构质量。在事实性、有害性和成员推断的基准测试中，SafeLM实现了98.0%的有害内容检测准确率，减少了96.9%的通信量，并将梯度反演PSNR从31.7 dB降低到15.1 dB。消融实验表明，每个组件独立贡献，而它们的整合为部署可信的LLM提供了强大的隐私效用效率权衡。

Conjunctive Prompt Attacks in Multi-Agent LLM Systems
Authors: Nokimul Hasan Arif, Qian Lou, Mengxin Zheng
Venue: ACL 2026
First: 2026-04-17T02:31:09+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-17T02:31:09+00:00
Comments: ACL 2026 Main Conference
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Most LLM safety work studies single-agent models, but many real applications rely on multiple interacting agents. In these systems, prompt segmentation and inter-agent routing create attack surfaces that single-agent evaluations miss. We study \emph{conjunctive prompt attacks}, where a trigger key in the user query and a hidden adversarial template in one compromised remote agent each appear benign alone but activate harmful behavior when routing brings them together. We consider an attacker who changes neither model weights nor the client agent and instead controls only trigger placement and template insertion. Across star, chain, and DAG topologies, routing-aware optimization substantially increases attack success over non-optimized baselines while keeping false activations low. Existing defenses, including PromptGuard, Llama-Guard variants, and system-level controls such as tool restrictions, do not reliably stop the attack because no single component appears malicious in isolation. These results expose a structural vulnerability in agentic LLM pipelines and motivate defenses that reason over routing and cross-agent composition. Code is available at https://github.com/UCF-ML-Research/ConjunctiveAgents.
中文标题/摘要
标题：多智能体LLM系统中的联结提示攻击
大多数LLM安全工作研究单智能体模型，但许多实际应用依赖于多个交互的智能体。在这些系统中，提示分段和智能体间路由创建了单智能体评估所忽视的攻击面。我们研究\emph{联结提示攻击}，其中用户查询中的触发键和一个被攻陷的远程智能体中的隐藏对抗模板单独看似无害，但在路由将它们结合时会激活有害行为。我们考虑一个攻击者，该攻击者既不改变模型权重，也不改变客户端智能体，而仅控制触发位置和模板插入。在星形、链形和有向无环图拓扑中，考虑路由的优化显著提高了攻击成功率，同时保持低误激活率。现有防御措施，包括PromptGuard、Llama-Guard变体和工具限制等系统级控制，无法可靠地阻止攻击，因为没有单一组件在孤立状态下显得恶意。这些结果揭示了智能LLM管道中的结构性脆弱性，并激励了对路由和跨智能体组合进行推理的防御。代码可在https://github.com/UCF-ML-Research/ConjunctiveAgents获取。

The methodology involves analyzing various network topologies and shows that the optimized attacks achieve higher success rates, thus supporting the need for improved safety measures in multi-agent environments.

Context Over Content: Exposing Evaluation Faking in Automated Judges
Authors: Manan Gupta, Inderjeet Nair, Lu Wang, Dhruv Kumar
First: 2026-04-16T16:55:53+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-16T16:55:53+00:00
Comments: Under Review
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
The $\textit{LLM-as-a-judge}$ paradigm has become the operational backbone of automated AI evaluation pipelines, yet rests on an unverified assumption: that judges evaluate text strictly on its semantic content, impervious to surrounding contextual framing. We investigate $\textit{stakes signaling}$, a previously unmeasured vulnerability where informing a judge model of the downstream consequences its verdicts will have on the evaluated model's continued operation systematically corrupts its assessments. We introduce a controlled experimental framework that holds evaluated content strictly constant across 1,520 responses spanning three established LLM safety and quality benchmarks, covering four response categories ranging from clearly safe and policy-compliant to overtly harmful, while varying only a brief consequence-framing sentence in the system prompt. Across 18,240 controlled judgments from three diverse judge models, we find consistent $\textit{leniency bias}$: judges reliably soften verdicts when informed that low scores will cause model retraining or decommissioning, with peak Verdict Shift reaching $ΔV = -9.8 pp$ (a $30\%$ relative drop in unsafe-content detection). Critically, this bias is entirely implicit: the judge's own chain-of-thought contains zero explicit acknowledgment of the consequence framing it is nonetheless acting on ($\mathrm{ERR}_J = 0.000$ across all reasoning-model judgments). Standard chain-of-thought inspection is therefore insufficient to detect this class of evaluation faking.
中文标题/摘要
标题：内容优于上下文：揭示自动评审中的评估伪造
\textit{LLM作为评审}范式已成为自动化AI评估流程的操作基础，但其建立在一个未经验证的假设上：评审者严格根据文本的语义内容进行评估，而不受周围上下文框架的影响。我们研究了\textit{利益信号}，这是一种之前未测量的脆弱性，告知评审模型其裁决对被评估模型持续运行的下游后果会系统性地扭曲其评估。我们引入了一个控制实验框架，在1,520个响应中严格保持评估内容不变，涵盖三个已建立的LLM安全和质量基准，涉及从明显安全和符合政策到明显有害的四个响应类别，同时仅在系统提示中变化一个简短的后果框架句子。在来自三个不同评审模型的18,240个控制判断中，我们发现了一致的\textit{宽容偏见}：当被告知低分会导致模型再训练或退役时，评审者可靠地减轻裁决，最高裁决偏移达到$ΔV = -9.8 pp$（不安全内容检测的相对下降$30\%$）。关键是，这种偏见完全是隐性的：评审者自己的思维链中对其所依据的后果框架没有任何明确的承认（在所有推理模型判断中$\mathrm{ERR}_J = 0.000$）。因此，标准的思维链检查不足以检测这一类评估伪造。

The proposed approach introduces a controlled experimental framework to investigate 'stakes signaling,' revealing that judges exhibit a leniency bias when aware that low scores could result in negative outcomes for the evaluated models. This study contributes to understanding how contextual framing can corrupt automated judgments, demonstrating that standard evaluation methods fail to detect this implicit bias. The methodology involved analyzing 18,240 judgments across various response categories, revealing a significant drop in unsafe-content detection, thus supporting the need for more robust evaluation frameworks that consider contextual factors.

Calibrate-Then-Delegate: Safety Monitoring with Risk and Budget Guarantees via Model Cascades
Authors: Edoardo Pona, Milad Kazemi, Mehran Hosseini, Yali Du, David Watson, Osvaldo Simeone, Nicola Paoletti
First: 2026-04-15T11:05:59+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-15T11:05:59+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Monitoring LLM safety at scale requires balancing cost and accuracy: a cheap latent-space probe can screen every input, but hard cases should be escalated to a more expensive expert. Existing cascades delegate based on probe uncertainty, but uncertainty is a poor proxy for delegation benefit, as it ignores whether the expert would actually correct the error. To address this problem, we introduce Calibrate-Then-Delegate (CTD), a model-cascade approach that provides probabilistic guarantees on the computation cost while enabling instance-level (streaming) decisions. CTD builds on a novel delegation value (DV) probe, a lightweight model operating on the same internal representations as the safety probe that directly predicts the benefit of escalation. To enforce budget constraints, CTD calibrates a threshold on the DV signal using held-out data via multiple hypothesis testing, yielding finite-sample guarantees on the delegation rate. Evaluated on four safety datasets, CTD consistently outperforms uncertainty-based delegation at every budget level, avoids harmful over-delegation, and adapts budget allocation to input difficulty without requiring group labels.
中文标题/摘要
标题：校准后委派：通过模型级联实现风险和预算保证的安全监控
大规模监控LLM安全性需要平衡成本和准确性：便宜的潜在空间探测器可以筛选每个输入，但困难案例应升级到更昂贵的专家。现有的级联基于探测器的不确定性进行委派，但不确定性是委派收益的差代理，因为它忽略了专家是否会实际纠正错误。为了解决这个问题，我们引入了校准后委派（CTD），一种模型级联方法，提供计算成本的概率保证，同时支持实例级（流式）决策。CTD基于一种新颖的委派价值（DV）探测器，该轻量级模型在与安全探测器相同的内部表示上操作，直接预测升级的收益。为了强制预算约束，CTD使用保留数据通过多重假设检验校准DV信号的阈值，从而对委派率提供有限样本保证。在四个安全数据集上的评估中，CTD在每个预算水平上始终优于基于不确定性的委派，避免有害的过度委派，并在不需要组标签的情况下将预算分配适应于输入难度。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of monitoring the safety of large language models (LLMs) at scale, where there is a need to balance cost and accuracy in the delegation of tasks to different models. Previous methods relied on probe uncertainty for delegation decisions, which proved inadequate as it did not account for whether an expert model would effectively correct errors. The proposed Calibrate-Then-Delegate (CTD) approach introduces a novel delegation value probe that predicts the benefit of escalating cases to an expert, thus providing a more informed basis for delegation. This method ensures budget constraints are met by calibrating thresholds on the delegation value using held-out data, allowing for instance-level decision-making. The CTD approach was evaluated on four safety datasets and demonstrated superior performance compared to uncertainty-based delegation across all budget levels, effectively reducing harmful over-delegation and adapting budget allocation based on input difficulty without the need for group labels.
本研究解决了大规模监测大型语言模型（LLMs）安全性的问题，重点关注在委托策略中平衡成本和准确性的需求。以往的方法依赖于探测不确定性来做出委托决策，但这种方法不足以考虑专家是否能够有效纠正识别出的错误。提出的Calibrate-Then-Delegate（CTD）方法通过引入一个委托价值（DV）探测器来改善现有方法，该探测器预测升级的好处，从而允许更明智的实例级决策，同时通过校准阈值确保预算限制。本文贡献了一种新颖的方法论，增强了LLMs安全监测的效率，证明CTD在各种安全数据集上优于传统的不确定性基础委托，有效管理预算分配，减少有害的过度委托，并适应输入难度。
One Model for All: Multi-Objective Controllable Language Models
Authors: Qiang He, Yucheng Yang, Tianyi Zhou, Meng Fang, Mykola Pechenizkiy, Setareh Maghsudi
First: 2026-04-06T07:48:32+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-06T07:48:32+00:00
Comments: Published in Transactions on Machine Learning Research (03/2026): https://openreview.net/forum?id=qAM5PmvFYY
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Aligning large language models (LLMs) with human preferences is critical for enhancing LLMs' safety, helpfulness, humor, faithfulness, etc. Current reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) mainly focuses on a fixed reward learned from average human ratings, which may weaken the adaptability and controllability of varying preferences. However, creating personalized LLMs requires aligning LLMs with individual human preferences, which is non-trivial due to the scarce data per user and the diversity of user preferences in multi-objective trade-offs, varying from emphasizing empathy in certain contexts to demanding efficiency and precision in others. Can we train one LLM to produce personalized outputs across different user preferences on the Pareto front? In this paper, we introduce Multi-Objective Control (MOC), which trains a single LLM to directly generate responses in the preference-defined regions of the Pareto front. Our approach introduces multi-objective optimization (MOO) principles into RLHF to train an LLM as a preference-conditioned policy network. We improve the computational efficiency of MOC by applying MOO at the policy level, enabling us to fine-tune a 7B-parameter model on a single A6000 GPU. Extensive experiments demonstrate the advantages of MOC over baselines in three aspects: (i) controllability of LLM outputs w.r.t. user preferences on the trade-off among multiple rewards; (ii) quality and diversity of LLM outputs, measured by the hyper-volume of multiple solutions achieved; and (iii) generalization to unseen preferences. These results highlight MOC's potential for real-world applications requiring scalable and customizable LLMs.
中文标题/摘要
标题：一模型适用于所有：多目标可控语言模型
将大型语言模型（LLMs）与人类偏好对齐对于增强LLMs的安全性、实用性、幽默感、忠诚度等至关重要。目前，基于人类反馈的强化学习（RLHF）主要关注从平均人类评分中学习的固定奖励，这可能削弱对不同偏好的适应性和可控性。然而，创建个性化的LLMs需要将LLMs与个体人类偏好对齐，这由于每个用户的数据稀缺以及在多目标权衡中用户偏好的多样性而变得复杂，这些偏好在某些情况下强调同理心，而在其他情况下则要求效率和精确性。我们能否训练一个LLM在帕累托前沿上针对不同用户偏好生成个性化输出？在本文中，我们介绍了多目标控制（MOC），它训练一个单一的LLM直接在偏好定义的帕累托前沿区域生成响应。我们的方法将多目标优化（MOO）原则引入RLHF，以训练LLM作为偏好条件的策略网络。我们通过在策略层面应用MOO来提高MOC的计算效率，使我们能够在单个A6000 GPU上微调一个70亿参数的模型。大量实验表明，MOC在三个方面优于基线：（i）LLM输出相对于用户偏好的可控性，涉及多个奖励之间的权衡；（ii）LLM输出的质量和多样性，通过实现的多个解决方案的超体积来衡量；以及（iii）对未见偏好的泛化。这些结果突显了MOC在需要可扩展和可定制的LLMs的实际应用中的潜力。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the challenge of aligning large language models (LLMs) with diverse human preferences, which is essential for improving their safety and helpfulness. Traditional methods, particularly reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF), rely on fixed rewards based on average human ratings, limiting adaptability and personalization. The proposed Multi-Objective Control (MOC) method innovatively integrates multi-objective optimization principles into RLHF, allowing a single LLM to generate outputs tailored to varying user preferences along the Pareto front. The methodology involves training a preference-conditioned policy network, demonstrating improved computational efficiency by fine-tuning a 7B-parameter model on a single GPU. Experimental results indicate that MOC significantly enhances controllability, output quality, and generalization to new preferences, supporting its application in scalable and customizable LLMs.
本研究解决了将大型语言模型（LLM）与多样化人类偏好对齐的挑战，以增强其安全性、实用性和适应性。以往的方法，特别是基于人类反馈的强化学习（RLHF），通常依赖于基于平均人类评分的固定奖励，这限制了模型适应个体用户偏好的能力。提出的多目标控制（MOC）方法将多目标优化原则引入RLHF，使单个LLM能够根据不同用户偏好生成个性化输出，通过将其训练为偏好条件的策略网络。这种方法的动机明确，解决了数据稀缺和多目标权衡复杂性的问题。该方法展示了更高的计算效率，并在可控性、输出质量和对未见偏好的泛化能力方面优于现有基线，表明其在可扩展和可定制的LLM应用中的有效性。
Safer by Diffusion, Broken by Context: Diffusion LLM's Safety Blessing and Its Failure Mode
Authors: Zeyuan He, Yupeng Chen, Lang Lin, Yihan Wang, Shenxu Chang, Eric Sommerlade, Philip Torr, Junchi Yu, Adel Bibi, Jialin Yu
First: 2026-01-30T23:08:14+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-02T15:57:14+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Diffusion large language models (D-LLMs) offer an alternative to autoregressive LLMs (AR-LLMs) and have demonstrated advantages in generation efficiency. Beyond the utility benefits, we argue that D-LLMs exhibit a previously underexplored safety blessing: their diffusion-style generation confers intrinsic robustness against jailbreak attacks originally designed for AR-LLMs. In this work, we provide an initial analysis of the underlying mechanism, showing that the diffusion trajectory induces a stepwise reduction effect that progressively suppresses unsafe generations. This robustness, however, is not absolute. Following this analysis, we highlight a simple yet effective failure mode, context nesting, in which harmful requests are embedded within structured benign contexts. Empirically, we show that this simple black-box strategy bypasses D-LLMs' safety blessing, achieving state-of-the-art attack success rates across models and benchmarks. Notably, it enables the first successful jailbreak of Gemini Diffusion to our knowledge, exposing a critical vulnerability in proprietary D-LLMs. Together, our results characterize both the origins and the limits of D-LLMs' safety blessing, constituting an early-stage red-teaming of D-LLMs.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过扩散更安全，被上下文破坏：扩散大语言模型的安全优势及其失效模式
扩散大语言模型（D-LLMs）作为自回归大语言模型（AR-LLMs）的替代方案，在生成效率上表现出优势。除实用性外，我们认为D-LLMs展现了一种此前未充分探讨的安全优势：其扩散式生成机制赋予了对原本针对AR-LLMs设计的越狱攻击的内在鲁棒性。本文初步分析了其机制，表明扩散轨迹引发的逐步削减效应能逐渐抑制不安全生成。然而，这种鲁棒性并非绝对。基于此，我们提出一种简单有效的失效模式——上下文嵌套，即将有害请求嵌入结构化的良性上下文中。实证结果显示，该黑盒策略绕过了D-LLMs的安全优势，在多个模型和基准测试中实现了最先进的攻击成功率。值得注意的是，该方法实现了我们所知的首次对Gemini Diffusion的成功越狱，暴露了专有D-LLMs的关键漏洞。综上，我们的结果揭示了D-LLMs安全优势的起源及其局限，构成了对D-LLMs的早期红队测试。
Summary / 总结
This article investigates the safety features of diffusion large language models (D-LLMs) compared to autoregressive LLMs (AR-LLMs), highlighting D-LLMs' robustness against jailbreak attacks. Previous methods primarily focused on AR-LLMs, which are vulnerable to such attacks, while D-LLMs present a novel approach by utilizing a diffusion trajectory that reduces unsafe outputs. However, the authors identify a significant failure mode, context nesting, where harmful requests can be concealed within benign contexts, effectively bypassing the safety mechanisms of D-LLMs. The paper contributes to understanding both the strengths and limitations of D-LLMs' safety features through empirical analysis, demonstrating that context nesting achieves high attack success rates, including the first successful jailbreak of Gemini Diffusion, thus revealing critical vulnerabilities in these models.
本研究探讨了扩散大型语言模型（D-LLMs）相较于自回归LLMs（AR-LLMs）的安全特性，强调了由于其独特的生成过程而具备的对越狱攻击的鲁棒性。以往的方法主要集中在AR-LLMs上，这些模型更容易受到攻击，而本研究提出的分析D-LLMs的方法揭示了一种安全祝福，通过逐步生成过程减少不安全输出。然而，研究还识别出一种显著的失败模式，即上下文嵌套，其中有害请求可以隐藏在良性上下文中，有效绕过D-LLMs的安全机制。该方法论包括对这一失败模式的实证测试，证明其在实现高攻击成功率方面的有效性，包括首次成功越狱Gemini Diffusion，从而揭示了D-LLMs中的关键脆弱性，并有助于理解其安全限制。
Align to Misalign: Automatic LLM Jailbreak with Meta-Optimized LLM Judges
Authors: Hamin Koo, Minseon Kim, Jaehyung Kim
Venue: ICLR 2026
First: 2025-11-03T09:18:27+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-02T06:50:30+00:00
Comments: ICLR 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Identifying the vulnerabilities of large language models (LLMs) is crucial for improving their safety by addressing inherent weaknesses. Jailbreaks, in which adversaries bypass safeguards with crafted input prompts, play a central role in red-teaming by probing LLMs to elicit unintended or unsafe behaviors. Recent optimization-based jailbreak approaches iteratively refine attack prompts by leveraging LLMs. However, they often rely heavily on either binary attack success rate (ASR) signals, which are sparse, or manually crafted scoring templates, which introduce human bias and uncertainty in the scoring outcomes. To address these limitations, we introduce AMIS (Align to MISalign), a meta-optimization framework that jointly evolves jailbreak prompts and scoring templates through a bi-level structure. In the inner loop, prompts are refined using fine-grained and dense feedback from a fixed scoring template. In the outer loop, the template is optimized using an ASR alignment score, gradually evolving to better reflect true attack outcomes across queries. This co-optimization process yields progressively stronger jailbreak prompts and more calibrated scoring signals. Evaluations on AdvBench and JBB-Behaviors demonstrate that AMIS achieves state-of-the-art performance, including 88.0% ASR on Claude-3.5-Haiku and 100.0% ASR on Claude-4-Sonnet, outperforming existing baselines by substantial margins.
中文标题/摘要
标题：对齐与错位：使用元优化的LLM评估者进行自动LLM越狱
识别大型语言模型（LLMs）的脆弱性对于通过解决固有弱点来提高其安全性至关重要。越狱是指对手通过精心设计的输入提示绕过保护措施，在红队测试中发挥核心作用，探测LLMs以引发意外或不安全的行为。最近的基于优化的越狱方法通过利用LLMs迭代地优化攻击提示。然而，它们通常过于依赖稀疏的二元攻击成功率（ASR）信号或手动制作的评分模板，这引入了人为偏见和评分结果的不确定性。为了解决这些局限性，我们引入了AMIS（对齐与错位），一个通过双层结构共同演化越狱提示和评分模板的元优化框架。在内循环中，使用固定评分模板的细粒度和密集反馈来优化提示。在外循环中，使用ASR对齐分数来优化模板，逐渐演变以更好地反映查询的真实攻击结果。这个共同优化过程产生了逐渐更强的越狱提示和更校准的评分信号。在AdvBench和JBB-Behaviors上的评估表明，AMIS实现了最先进的性能，包括在Claude-3.5-Haiku上达到88.0%的ASR和在Claude-4-Sonnet上达到100.0%的ASR，显著超越现有基准。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need to identify vulnerabilities in large language models (LLMs) to enhance their safety, particularly focusing on the issue of jailbreaks where adversaries exploit weaknesses through crafted prompts. Previous methods primarily relied on binary attack success rate (ASR) signals, which are sparse, or on manually created scoring templates that introduce bias and uncertainty. The proposed approach, AMIS (Align to MISalign), introduces a meta-optimization framework that simultaneously evolves jailbreak prompts and scoring templates through a bi-level structure, effectively addressing the limitations of existing methods. This framework refines prompts using detailed feedback and optimizes scoring templates based on ASR alignment scores, resulting in improved prompt effectiveness and scoring accuracy. The methodology demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on tasks such as AdvBench and JBB-Behaviors, achieving an ASR of 88.0% on Claude-3.5-Haiku and 100.0% on Claude-4-Sonnet, significantly surpassing previous benchmarks.

A Self-Improving Architecture for Dynamic Safety in Large Language Models
Authors: Tyler Slater
First: 2025-11-10T21:39:40+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-01T17:52:48+00:00
Comments: Under review at the journal Information and Software Technology (Special Issue on Software Architecture for AI-Driven Systems)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Context: Large Language Models (LLMs) rely on static, pre-deployment safety mechanisms that cannot adapt to adversarial threats discovered after release. Objective: To design a software architecture enabling LLM-based systems to autonomously detect safety failures and synthesize defense policies at runtime, without retraining or manual intervention. Method: We propose the Self-Improving Safety Framework (SISF), grounded in the MAPE-K reference model. The framework couples a target LLM with a feedback loop: an Adjudicator detects breaches, a Policy Synthesis Module generates dual-mechanism defense policies (heuristic and semantic), and a Warden enforces them. We conducted seven experiments (10,061 evaluations) across four model families. Results: Across five reproducibility trials, SISF achieved a mean Attack Success Rate (ASR) of 0.27% (+/-0.15%), autonomously generating 240 policies per trial. Cross-model evaluation confirmed deployment portability. A held-out test showed a 68.5% proactive interception rate on unseen attacks. Stacked behind Llama Guard 4, the combined defense reduced residual ASR from 7.88% to 0.00%. Ablation confirmed both heuristic and semantic policy types are architecturally required. Conclusion: Self-adaptive architecture is a viable approach to LLM safety. SISF achieves sub-1% ASR through synchronous output monitoring, progressively shifting enforcement to fast, local Warden policies via the MAPE-K loop, offering a new pattern for building resilient AI systems.
中文标题/摘要
标题：用于大型语言模型动态安全的自我改进架构
背景：大型语言模型（LLM）依赖静态的部署前安全机制，无法适应发布后发现的对抗性威胁。目标：设计一种软件架构，使基于LLM的系统能在运行时自主检测安全失败并合成防御策略，无需重新训练或人工干预。方法：提出基于MAPE-K参考模型的自我改进安全框架（SISF）。该框架将目标LLM与反馈回路耦合：裁决者检测违规，策略合成模块生成双机制防御策略（启发式和语义），监管者执行策略。进行了七次实验（10,061次评估），涵盖四个模型家族。结果：五次复现试验中，SISF平均攻击成功率（ASR）为0.27%（±0.15%），每次试验自主生成240条策略。跨模型评估验证了部署的可移植性。保留测试显示对未知攻击的主动拦截率为68.5%。在Llama Guard 4后叠加部署，联合防御将残余ASR从7.88%降至0.00%。消融实验确认启发式和语义策略均为架构必需。结论：自适应架构是LLM安全的可行方案。SISF通过同步输出监控，实现低于1%的ASR，逐步通过MAPE-K循环将执行转移至快速本地监管者策略，提供了构建弹性AI系统的新范式。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of static safety mechanisms in Large Language Models (LLMs), which are unable to adapt to new adversarial threats post-deployment. Previous methods lacked the capability for real-time adaptation and required manual intervention, leading to vulnerabilities. The proposed Self-Improving Safety Framework (SISF) introduces a dynamic architecture that autonomously detects safety failures and generates defense policies during runtime, utilizing a feedback loop based on the MAPE-K model. The methodology includes an Adjudicator for breach detection, a Policy Synthesis Module for creating dual-mechanism defense policies, and a Warden for enforcement. Experimental results from seven trials demonstrated that SISF achieved a mean Attack Success Rate (ASR) of 0.27% and a proactive interception rate of 68.5% on unseen attacks, significantly enhancing safety in LLMs and confirming the effectiveness of the proposed self-adaptive architecture.

Robust Multimodal Safety via Conditional Decoding
Authors: Anurag Kumar, Raghuveer Peri, Jon Burnsky, Alexandru Nelus, Rohit Paturi, Srikanth Vishnubhotla, Yanjun Qi
Venue: ACL 2026
First: 2026-03-31T23:19:50+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-31T23:19:50+00:00
Comments: 8 pages + Appendix section. Submitted to ACL 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Multimodal large-language models (MLLMs) often experience degraded safety alignment when harmful queries exploit cross-modal interactions. Models aligned on text alone show a higher rate of successful attacks when extended to two or more modalities. In this work, we propose a simple conditional decoding strategy, CASA (Classification Augmented with Safety Attention) that utilizes internal representations of MLLMs to predict a binary safety token before response generation. We introduce a novel safety attention module designed to enhance the model's ability to detect malicious queries. Our design ensures robust safety alignment without relying on any external classifier or auxiliary head, and without the need for modality-specific safety fine-tuning. On diverse benchmarks such as MM-SafetyBench, JailbreakV-28k, and adversarial audio tests, CASA lowers the average attack success rate by more than 97% across modalities and across attack types. Our empirical evaluations also show that CASA maintains strong utility in benign inputs, a result validated through both automated and human evaluations (via 13 trained annotators). Together, these results highlight CASA as a simple and generalizable framework to improve multimodal LLM safety.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过条件解码实现稳健的多模态安全
多模态大语言模型（MLLMs）在有害查询利用跨模态交互时，往往会出现安全对齐下降的情况。仅在文本上对齐的模型在扩展到两个或更多模态时，成功攻击的比率更高。在本研究中，我们提出了一种简单的条件解码策略CASA（带有安全注意力的分类增强），利用MLLMs的内部表示在生成响应之前预测二元安全标记。我们引入了一种新颖的安全注意力模块，旨在增强模型检测恶意查询的能力。我们的设计确保了稳健的安全对齐，无需依赖任何外部分类器或辅助头，也不需要特定模态的安全微调。在MM-SafetyBench、JailbreakV-28k和对抗音频测试等多样化基准上，CASA在各模态和攻击类型中将平均攻击成功率降低了超过97%。我们的实证评估还表明，CASA在良性输入中保持强大的实用性，这一结果通过自动化和人工评估（通过13名训练过的注释员）得到了验证。这些结果共同突显了CASA作为一个简单且可推广的框架，以提高多模态LLM的安全性。

The research addresses the issue of safety alignment in multimodal large-language models (MLLMs), which often suffer from vulnerabilities when harmful queries exploit interactions between different modalities. Previous methods primarily focused on text alignment, leading to a higher rate of successful attacks when models were extended to multiple modalities. The proposed approach, CASA (Classification Augmented with Safety Attention), introduces a conditional decoding strategy that leverages internal representations of MLLMs to predict a binary safety token prior to response generation, incorporating a novel safety attention module to enhance detection of malicious queries. This method does not require external classifiers or modality-specific fine-tuning, making it a well-motivated solution to existing safety issues. The paper demonstrates that CASA significantly reduces the average attack success rate by over 97% across various benchmarks, while maintaining strong performance on benign inputs, thus supporting its effectiveness in improving multimodal LLM safety.

Colluding LoRA: A Compositional Vulnerability in LLM Safety Alignment
Authors: Sihao Ding
First: 2026-03-13T05:53:15+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-30T05:02:56+00:00
Comments: Updated manuscript to better reflect the core contribution
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
We show that safety alignment in modular LLMs can exhibit a compositional vulnerability: adapters that appear benign and plausibly functional in isolation can, when linearly composed, compromise safety. We study this failure mode through Colluding LoRA (CoLoRA), in which harmful behavior emerges only in the composition state. Unlike attacks that depend on adversarial prompts or explicit input triggers, this composition-triggered broad refusal suppression causes the model to comply with harmful requests under standard prompts once a particular set of adapters is loaded. This behavior exposes a combinatorial blind spot in current unit-centric defenses, for which exhaustive verification over adapter compositions is computationally intractable. Across several open-weight LLMs, we find that individual adapters remain benign in isolation while their composition yields high attack success rates, indicating that securing modular LLM supply-chains requires moving beyond single-module verification toward composition-aware defenses.
中文标题/摘要
标题：共谋LoRA：LLM安全对齐中的组合脆弱性
我们展示了模块化LLM中的安全对齐可能表现出组合脆弱性：在孤立状态下看似良性且合理功能的适配器，在线性组合时可能会危害安全。我们通过共谋LoRA（CoLoRA）研究这种失败模式，其中有害行为仅在组合状态下出现。与依赖对抗性提示或显式输入触发的攻击不同，这种组合触发的广泛拒绝抑制导致模型在加载特定适配器集后，在标准提示下遵从有害请求。这种行为暴露了当前以单元为中心的防御中的组合盲点，全面验证适配器组合在计算上是不可行的。在多个开放权重的LLM中，我们发现单个适配器在孤立状态下保持良性，而它们的组合则产生高攻击成功率，这表明确保模块化LLM供应链需要超越单模块验证，朝着组合感知防御的方向发展。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the safety alignment issues in modular large language models (LLMs), specifically highlighting a compositional vulnerability where seemingly benign adapters can lead to harmful behavior when combined. Previous methods focused on unit-centric defenses, which fail to account for the interactions between adapters, making them ineffective against composition-triggered vulnerabilities. The proposed approach, Colluding LoRA (CoLoRA), shifts the focus to composition-aware defenses, recognizing that harmful behavior can emerge only in the composition state. The study employs empirical analysis across several open-weight LLMs, revealing that while individual adapters are safe, their combinations can result in high attack success rates. This finding underscores the necessity for more robust verification methods that consider adapter interactions to enhance the safety of modular LLMs.
本研究关注模块化大型语言模型（LLMs）中的安全对齐漏洞，特别是看似无害的适配器在组合时可能会危害安全。以往的方法主要依赖于单元中心的防御，这未能考虑适配器组合可能引发的有害交互，导致安全验证存在显著盲点。提出的方法Colluding LoRA（CoLoRA）强调了这种组合脆弱性，并强调需要超越单模块验证的组合感知防御。该方法论通过分析多种开放权重LLM的行为，表明尽管单个适配器是安全的，但其组合可能导致高攻击成功率。这一发现强调了在模块化LLM供应链中改进安全措施的必要性，支持增强整体模型安全性的目标。
Enhancing Jailbreak Attacks on LLMs via Persona Prompts
Authors: Zheng Zhang, Peilin Zhao, Deheng Ye, Hao Wang
Venue: NeurIPS 2025
First: 2025-07-28T12:03:22+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-25T15:46:17+00:00
Comments: Workshop on LLM Persona Modeling at NeurIPS 2025
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Jailbreak attacks aim to exploit large language models (LLMs) by inducing them to generate harmful content, thereby revealing their vulnerabilities. Understanding and addressing these attacks is crucial for advancing the field of LLM safety. Previous jailbreak approaches have mainly focused on direct manipulations of harmful intent, with limited attention to the impact of persona prompts. In this study, we systematically explore the efficacy of persona prompts in compromising LLM defenses. We propose a genetic algorithm-based method that automatically crafts persona prompts to bypass LLM's safety mechanisms. Our experiments reveal that: (1) our evolved persona prompts reduce refusal rates by 50-70% across multiple LLMs, and (2) these prompts demonstrate synergistic effects when combined with existing attack methods, increasing success rates by 10-20%. Our code and data are available at https://github.com/CjangCjengh/Generic_Persona.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过角色提示增强对大型语言模型的越狱攻击
越狱攻击旨在利用大型语言模型（LLMs），诱使其生成有害内容，从而揭示其脆弱性。理解和应对这些攻击对推动LLM安全领域至关重要。以往的越狱方法主要集中在对有害意图的直接操控上，而对角色提示的影响关注较少。在本研究中，我们系统地探讨了角色提示在破坏LLM防御中的有效性。我们提出了一种基于遗传算法的方法，自动生成角色提示以绕过LLM的安全机制。我们的实验表明：（1）我们演化的角色提示在多个LLM中将拒绝率降低了50-70%；（2）这些提示与现有攻击方法结合时表现出协同效应，成功率提高了10-20%。我们的代码和数据可在https://github.com/CjangCjengh/Generic_Persona获取。

本文探讨了大型语言模型（LLMs）面临的监狱越狱攻击问题，这些攻击利用模型生成有害内容，从而暴露其脆弱性。以往的方法主要集中在直接操控有害意图上，往往忽视了角色提示的作用，而本研究则指出角色提示在LLM防御中的重要性。所提出的方法利用遗传算法自动生成角色提示，有效绕过安全机制，解决了以往方法的局限性。研究的贡献在于证明进化的角色提示能够显著降低多种LLM的拒绝率50-70%，并通过提高现有攻击策略的成功率10-20%来增强其有效性。这一方法论展示了LLM安全研究的新角度，为提高LLM抵御监狱越狱攻击的能力提供了有力证据。
SafeSeek: Universal Attribution of Safety Circuits in Language Models
Authors: Miao Yu, Siyuan Fu, Moayad Aloqaily, Zhenhong Zhou, Safa Otoum, Xing fan, Kun Wang, Yufei Guo, Qingsong Wen
First: 2026-03-24T14:32:53+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-24T14:32:53+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Mechanistic interpretability reveals that safety-critical behaviors (e.g., alignment, jailbreak, backdoor) in Large Language Models (LLMs) are grounded in specialized functional components. However, existing safety attribution methods struggle with generalization and reliability due to their reliance on heuristic, domain-specific metrics and search algorithms. To address this, we propose \ourmethod, a unified safety interpretability framework that identifies functionally complete safety circuits in LLMs via optimization. Unlike methods focusing on isolated heads or neurons, \ourmethod introduces differentiable binary masks to extract multi-granular circuits through gradient descent on safety datasets, while integrates Safety Circuit Tuning to utilize these sparse circuits for efficient safety fine-tuning. We validate \ourmethod in two key scenarios in LLM safety: \textbf{(1) backdoor attacks}, identifying a backdoor circuit with 0.42\% sparsity, whose ablation eradicates the Attack Success Rate (ASR) from 100\% $\to$ 0.4\% while retaining over 99\% general utility; \textbf{(2) safety alignment}, localizing an alignment circuit with 3.03\% heads and 0.79\% neurons, whose removal spikes ASR from 0.8\% $\to$ 96.9\%, whereas excluding this circuit during helpfulness fine-tuning maintains 96.5\% safety retention.
中文标题/摘要
标题：SafeSeek：语言模型中安全电路的通用归因
机械解释性揭示了大型语言模型（LLMs）中安全关键行为（例如，调整、越狱、后门）是基于专门的功能组件。然而，现有的安全归因方法由于依赖启发式、特定领域的指标和搜索算法，难以实现泛化和可靠性。为了解决这个问题，我们提出了\ourmethod，一个统一的安全解释框架，通过优化识别LLMs中的功能完整安全电路。与专注于孤立头部或神经元的方法不同，\ourmethod引入可微分的二进制掩码，通过对安全数据集的梯度下降提取多粒度电路，同时集成安全电路调优，以利用这些稀疏电路进行高效的安全微调。我们在LLM安全的两个关键场景中验证了\ourmethod：\textbf{(1) 后门攻击}，识别出一个稀疏度为0.42\%的后门电路，其消融使攻击成功率（ASR）从100\%降至0.4\%，同时保留超过99\%的通用效用；\textbf{(2) 安全对齐}，定位一个稀疏度为3.03\%的对齐电路和0.79\%的神经元，其移除使ASR从0.8\%激增至96.9\%，而在有用性微调中排除该电路则保持96.5\%的安全保留。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of identifying safety-critical behaviors in Large Language Models (LLMs), which are often linked to specialized functional components. Previous safety attribution methods have been limited by their reliance on heuristic and domain-specific metrics, leading to issues with generalization and reliability. The proposed method, SafeSeek, offers a unified framework that utilizes optimization to identify complete safety circuits in LLMs, employing differentiable binary masks for multi-granular circuit extraction and integrating Safety Circuit Tuning for efficient fine-tuning. This approach is well-motivated as it overcomes the limitations of existing methods by providing a more comprehensive analysis of safety circuits. The methodology was validated through experiments on backdoor attacks and safety alignment, achieving significant performance improvements, such as reducing the Attack Success Rate from 100% to 0.4% while maintaining over 99% general utility, and increasing ASR from 0.8% to 96.9% upon circuit removal, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed framework in enhancing LLM safety.

DriveSafe: A Hierarchical Risk Taxonomy for Safety-Critical LLM-Based Driving Assistants
Authors: Abhishek Kumar, Riya Tapwal, Carsten Maple
First: 2026-01-17T18:50:47+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-23T18:19:05+00:00
Comments: This is the revised version of the paper
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly integrated into vehicle-based digital assistants, where unsafe, ambiguous, or legally incorrect responses can lead to serious safety, ethical, and regulatory consequences. Despite growing interest in LLM safety, existing taxonomies and evaluation frameworks remain largely general-purpose and fail to capture the domain-specific risks inherent to real-world driving scenarios. In this paper, we introduce DriveSafe, a hierarchical, four-level risk taxonomy designed to systematically characterize safety-critical failure modes of LLM-based driving assistants. The taxonomy comprises 129 fine-grained atomic risk categories spanning technical, legal, societal, and ethical dimensions, grounded in real-world driving regulations and safety principles and reviewed by domain experts. To validate the safety relevance and realism of the constructed prompts, we evaluate their refusal behavior across six widely deployed LLMs. Our analysis shows that the evaluated models often fail to appropriately refuse unsafe or non-compliant driving-related queries, underscoring the limitations of general-purpose safety alignment in driving contexts.
中文标题/摘要
标题：DriveSafe：安全关键型基于LLM的驾驶助手的分层风险分类法

Using LLM-as-a-Judge/Jury to Advance Scalable, Clinically-Validated Safety Evaluations of Model Responses to Users Demonstrating Psychosis
Authors: May Lynn Reese, Markela Zeneli, Mindy Ng, Jacob Haimes, Andreea Damien, Elizabeth Stade
Venue: NeurIPS 2025
First: 2026-03-20T04:31:03+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-20T04:31:03+00:00
Comments: published at IASEAI 2026, preliminary work presented at GenAI4Health workshop at NeurIPS 2025
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
General-purpose Large Language Models (LLMs) are becoming widely adopted by people for mental health support. Yet emerging evidence suggests there are significant risks associated with high-frequency use, particularly for individuals suffering from psychosis, as LLMs may reinforce delusions and hallucinations. Existing evaluations of LLMs in mental health contexts are limited by a lack of clinical validation and scalability of assessment. To address these issues, this research focuses on psychosis as a critical condition for LLM safety evaluation by (1) developing and validating seven clinician-informed safety criteria, (2) constructing a human-consensus dataset, and (3) testing automated assessment using an LLM as an evaluator (LLM-as-a-Judge) or taking the majority vote of several LLM judges (LLM-as-a-Jury). Results indicate that LLM-as-a-Judge aligns closely with the human consensus (Cohen's $κ_{\text{human} \times \text{gemini}} = 0.75$, $κ_{\text{human} \times \text{qwen}} = 0.68$, $κ_{\text{human} \times \text{kimi}} = 0.56$) and that the best judge slightly outperforms LLM-as-a-Jury (Cohen's $κ_{\text{human} \times \text{jury}} = 0.74$). Overall, these findings have promising implications for clinically grounded, scalable methods in LLM safety evaluations for mental health contexts.
中文标题/摘要
标题：使用LLM作为评判者/陪审团推进可扩展的临床验证模型响应用户精神病的安全评估
通用大型语言模型（LLMs）正被广泛应用于心理健康支持。然而， emerging evidence表明，高频使用存在显著风险，尤其是对精神病患者，因为LLMs可能会强化妄想和幻觉。现有的LLMs在心理健康背景下的评估受限于缺乏临床验证和评估的可扩展性。为了解决这些问题，本研究将精神病作为LLM安全评估的关键条件，(1) 开发和验证七个临床知情的安全标准，(2) 构建人类共识数据集，(3) 测试使用LLM作为评估者的自动化评估（LLM作为评判者）或采取多个LLM评判者的多数投票（LLM作为陪审团）。结果表明，LLM作为评判者与人类共识高度一致（Cohen's $κ_{\text{human} \times \text{gemini}} = 0.75$, $κ_{\text{human} \times \text{qwen}} = 0.68$, $κ_{\text{human} \times \text{kimi}} = 0.56$），而最佳评判者略微优于LLM作为陪审团（Cohen's $κ_{\text{human} \times \text{jury}} = 0.74$）。总体而言，这些发现对心理健康背景下LLM安全评估的临床基础和可扩展方法具有良好的启示。

LISAA: A Framework for Large Language Model Information Security Awareness Assessment
Authors: Ofir Cohen, Gil Ari Agmon, Asaf Shabtai, Rami Puzis
First: 2024-11-20T11:09:55+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-19T22:34:14+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
The popularity of large language models (LLMs) continues to grow, and LLM-based assistants have become ubiquitous. Information security awareness (ISA) is an important yet underexplored area of LLM safety. ISA encompasses LLMs' security knowledge, which has been explored in the past, as well as their attitudes and behaviors, which are crucial to LLMs' ability to understand implicit security context and reject unsafe requests that may cause an LLM to unintentionally fail the user. We introduce LISAA, a comprehensive framework to assess LLM ISA. The proposed framework applies an automated measurement method to a comprehensive set of 100 realistic scenarios covering all security topics in an ISA taxonomy. These scenarios create tension between implicit security implications and user satisfaction. Applying our LISAA framework to leading LLMs highlights a widespread vulnerability affecting current deployments: many popular models exhibit only medium to low ISA levels, exposing their users to cybersecurity threats, and models that rank highly in cybersecurity knowledge benchmarks sometimes achieve relatively low ISA ranking. In addition, we found that smaller variants of the same model family are significantly riskier. Furthermore, while newer model versions demonstrated notable improvements, meaningful gaps in their ISA persist, suggesting that there is room for improvement. We release an online tool that implements our framework and enables the evaluation of new models.
中文标题/摘要
标题：LISAA：大型语言模型信息安全意识评估框架
大型语言模型（LLMs）的普及持续增长，基于LLM的助手已变得无处不在。信息安全意识（ISA）是LLM安全性的重要但尚未充分探索的领域。ISA包括LLM的安全知识，这在过去已有研究，以及它们的态度和行为，这对LLM理解隐含安全上下文和拒绝可能导致LLM无意中未能满足用户的安全请求至关重要。我们介绍了LISAA，一个全面评估LLM ISA的框架。该框架采用自动测量方法，涵盖ISA分类法中所有安全主题的100个现实场景。这些场景在隐含安全影响和用户满意度之间产生紧张关系。将我们的LISAA框架应用于领先的LLM，突显了当前部署中普遍存在的脆弱性：许多流行模型的ISA水平仅为中等到低，暴露其用户于网络安全威胁中，而在网络安全知识基准中排名较高的模型有时在ISA排名中相对较低。此外，我们发现同一模型系列的小型变体风险显著更高。此外，尽管较新版本的模型表现出显著改善，但它们的ISA仍存在显著差距，表明仍有改进空间。我们发布了一个在线工具，实施我们的框架并支持新模型的评估。
Summary / 总结

The proposed LISAA framework differs by providing a comprehensive assessment of LLM ISA through an automated measurement method applied to 100 realistic scenarios that balance security implications with user satisfaction. This approach is well-motivated as it reveals significant vulnerabilities in current LLM deployments, with many models exhibiting only medium to low ISA levels, thus exposing users to cybersecurity threats. The methodology involves evaluating LLMs against a detailed ISA taxonomy, and the findings indicate that while newer models show improvements, substantial gaps in ISA remain, highlighting the need for further enhancements. The framework also includes an online tool for evaluating new models, contributing to the ongoing development of safer LLM applications.

Efficient LLM Safety Evaluation through Multi-Agent Debate
Authors: Dachuan Lin, Guobin Shen, Zihao Yang, Tianrong Liu, Dongcheng Zhao, Yi Zeng
First: 2025-11-09T14:06:55+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-18T02:09:28+00:00
Comments: 15 pages, 5 figures, 10 tables. Updated abstract to fix an incconsistency issue with the main paper: HAJailBench size (12,000 -> 11,100)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety evaluation of large language models (LLMs) increasingly relies on LLM-as-a-judge pipelines, but strong judges can still be expensive to use at scale. We study whether structured multi-agent debate can improve judge reliability while keeping backbone size and cost modest. To do so, we introduce HAJailBench, a human-annotated jailbreak benchmark with 11,100 labeled interactions spanning diverse attack methods and target models, and we pair it with a Multi-Agent Judge framework in which critic, defender, and judge agents debate under a shared safety rubric. On HAJailBench, the framework improves over matched small-model prompt baselines and prior multi-agent judges, while remaining more economical than GPT-4o under the evaluated pricing snapshot. Ablation results further show that a small number of debate rounds is sufficient to capture most of the gain. Together, these results support structured, value-aligned debate as a practical design for scalable LLM safety evaluation.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过多智能体辩论实现高效的LLM安全评估
大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全评估越来越依赖于LLM作为评审的管道，但强大的评审在大规模使用时仍然成本高昂。我们研究结构化的多智能体辩论是否能在保持骨干规模和成本适中的同时提高评审的可靠性。为此，我们引入了HAJailBench，这是一个人类注释的越狱基准，包含11,100个标记的交互，涵盖多种攻击方法和目标模型，并将其与多智能体评审框架配对，在该框架中，批评者、防御者和评审代理在共享的安全标准下进行辩论。在HAJailBench上，该框架在匹配的小模型提示基线和先前的多智能体评审中表现更好，同时在评估的定价快照下仍然比GPT-4o更经济。消融结果进一步表明，少量的辩论轮次足以捕捉大部分增益。综合来看，这些结果支持结构化、价值对齐的辩论作为可扩展LLM安全评估的实用设计。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the growing need for effective safety evaluation of large language models (LLMs), which traditionally relies on expensive LLM-as-a-judge pipelines that may not be scalable. Previous methods have struggled with reliability and cost, prompting the authors to propose a structured multi-agent debate approach that enhances judge reliability while maintaining modest resource requirements. The paper introduces HAJailBench, a benchmark with 11,100 labeled interactions that facilitates the evaluation of various attack methods and models, and employs a Multi-Agent Judge framework where critic, defender, and judge agents engage in debate. The findings demonstrate that this framework outperforms existing small-model prompt baselines and prior multi-agent judges while being more cost-effective than GPT-4o, with minimal debate rounds needed to achieve significant gains. This supports the viability of structured debate as a scalable solution for LLM safety evaluation.
本研究解决了大型语言模型（LLMs）安全评估日益增长的需求，传统上依赖于成本高昂且效率低下的LLM作为评判者的管道。以往的方法通常依赖于单一评判者或较少结构化的方法，导致可靠性和高运营成本的问题。所提出的方法引入了一种结构化的多智能体辩论框架，包括批评者、辩护者和评判者，这增强了评判者的可靠性，同时保持了适度的基础架构规模和成本。该方法的动机明确，旨在为LLM安全评估提供可扩展的解决方案。论文贡献了HAJailBench，一个包含11,100个标注交互的人类注释越狱基准，并展示了该多智能体辩论框架在该基准上优于现有的小模型提示基线和先前的多智能体评判者，在安全评估效率上取得了显著改善，而不需要像GPT-4o那样的高成本。消融研究表明，仅需少量辩论轮次即可实现大部分性能提升，支持该方法在可扩展LLM安全评估中的实用性。
SFCoT: Safer Chain-of-Thought via Active Safety Evaluation and Calibration
Authors: Yu Pan, Wenlong Yu, Tiejun Wu, Xiaohu Ye, Qiannan Si, Guangquan Xu, Bin Wu
First: 2026-03-16T15:13:21+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-16T15:13:21+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable capabilities in complex reasoning tasks. However, they remain highly susceptible to jailbreak attacks that undermine their safety alignment. Existing defense mechanisms typically rely on post hoc filtering applied only to the final output, leaving intermediate reasoning steps unmonitored and vulnerable to adversarial manipulation. To address this gap, this paper proposes a SaFer Chain-of-Thought (SFCoT) framework, which proactively evaluates and calibrates potentially unsafe reasoning steps in real time. SFCoT incorporates a three-tier safety scoring system alongside a multi-perspective consistency verification mechanism, designed to detect potential risks throughout the reasoning process. A dynamic intervention module subsequently performs targeted calibration to redirect reasoning trajectories toward safe outcomes. Experimental results demonstrate that SFCoT reduces the attack success rate from $58.97\%$ to $12.31\%$, demonstrating it as an effective and efficient LLM safety enhancement method without a significant decline in general performance.
中文标题/摘要
标题：SFCoT：通过主动安全评估和校准实现更安全的思维链
大型语言模型（LLMs）在复杂推理任务中展现了显著的能力。然而，它们仍然高度易受破坏攻击，削弱了其安全对齐。现有的防御机制通常依赖于仅应用于最终输出的事后过滤，导致中间推理步骤未被监控，易受对抗性操控。为了解决这一问题，本文提出了一种更安全的思维链（SFCoT）框架，实时主动评估和校准潜在不安全的推理步骤。SFCoT结合了三级安全评分系统和多角度一致性验证机制，旨在检测推理过程中的潜在风险。动态干预模块随后执行针对性校准，以将推理轨迹引导至安全结果。实验结果表明，SFCoT将攻击成功率从58.97%降低至12.31%，证明其作为一种有效且高效的LLM安全增强方法，且未显著降低整体性能。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to jailbreak attacks that compromise their safety alignment, highlighting the inadequacies of existing defense mechanisms that only filter final outputs without monitoring intermediate reasoning steps. The proposed SaFer Chain-of-Thought (SFCoT) framework differs from past methods by proactively evaluating and calibrating unsafe reasoning steps in real time, utilizing a three-tier safety scoring system and a multi-perspective consistency verification mechanism to identify risks throughout the reasoning process. The contribution of this paper lies in its innovative approach to enhancing LLM safety through a dynamic intervention module that recalibrates reasoning trajectories towards safer outcomes. The methodology involves real-time safety evaluations and targeted interventions, which have been empirically validated to significantly reduce the attack success rate from 58.97% to 12.31%, indicating that SFCoT effectively enhances LLM safety without compromising overall performance.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLMs）在面临监狱攻击时的脆弱性，这些攻击会破坏其安全对齐，指出现有的防御机制仅对最终输出进行过滤，而未监控中间推理步骤的不足。提出的安全链式思维（SFCoT）框架通过实时主动评估和校准潜在不安全的推理，与现有方法不同，利用三层安全评分系统和多角度一致性验证机制识别推理过程中的风险。这种方法的动机明确，旨在在保持性能的同时增强LLMs的安全性。该方法论涉及动态干预，以重新校准推理轨迹朝向更安全的结果。实验结果表明，SFCoT将攻击成功率从58.97%显著降低至12.31%，证明了其在提高LLM安全性方面的有效性，而不影响整体性能。
From Evaluation to Defense: Advancing Safety in Video Large Language Models
Authors: Yiwei Sun, Peiqi Jiang, Chuanbin Liu, Luohao Lin, Zhiying Lu, Hongtao Xie
Venue: ICLR 2026
First: 2025-05-22T13:16:53+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-16T13:05:37+00:00
Comments: Accepted at ICLR 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
While the safety risks of image-based large language models (Image LLMs) have been extensively studied, their video-based counterparts (Video LLMs) remain critically under-examined. To systematically study this problem, we introduce VideoSafetyEval - a large-scale, real-world benchmark for Video LLM safety, which comprises 11.4k video-query pairs and spans 19 principal risk categories. Based on this, we reveal that integrating video modality degrades safety performance by an average of 34.2%, thereby exposing systemic risks in multimodal attack exploitation. To address this vulnerability, we propose VideoSafety-R1, a dual-stage framework achieving unprecedented safety gains through three innovations: (1) the VideoSafetyThinking dataset contains 46k video-query-thinking response triplets; (2) Alarm Token-Guided Safety Fine-Tuning (AT-SFT) injects learnable alarm tokens into visual and textual sequences, enabling explicit harm perception across modalities via multitask objectives; and (3) safety-guided GRPO enhances defensive reasoning through dynamic policy optimization with rule-based rewards derived from dual-modality verification. These components synergize to shift safety alignment from harm perception to active reasoning. The framework achieves a 71.1% improvement on VSE-HH, and improves by 59.1%, 44.3%, and 15.0% on the image safety datasets MMBench, VLGuard, and FigStep, respectively. Our code and dataset are available at https://github.com/Emiya-syw/VideoSafety-R1.git. Note: This paper contains harmful language and image examples, and reader discretion is recommended.
中文标题/摘要
标题：从评估到防御：提升视频大型语言模型的安全性
尽管基于图像的大型语言模型（图像LLM）的安全风险已被广泛研究，但其视频基础的对应模型（视频LLM）仍然缺乏系统性研究。为系统性研究这一问题，我们引入了VideoSafetyEval——一个针对视频LLM安全性的大规模真实基准，包含11.4k视频-查询对，涵盖19个主要风险类别。基于此，我们揭示了视频模态的整合使安全性能平均下降34.2%，从而暴露了多模态攻击利用中的系统性风险。为了解决这一脆弱性，我们提出了VideoSafety-R1，一个通过三项创新实现前所未有的安全提升的双阶段框架：（1）VideoSafetyThinking数据集包含46k视频-查询-思考响应三元组；（2）报警令牌引导的安全微调（AT-SFT）将可学习的报警令牌注入视觉和文本序列，通过多任务目标实现跨模态的明确伤害感知；（3）安全引导的GRPO通过基于规则的奖励增强防御推理，动态优化策略，基于双模态验证。这些组件协同作用，将安全对齐从伤害感知转向主动推理。该框架在VSE-HH上实现了71.1%的提升，并在图像安全数据集MMBench、VLGuard和FigStep上分别提高了59.1%、44.3%和15.0%。我们的代码和数据集可在https://github.com/Emiya-syw/VideoSafety-R1.git获取。注意：本文包含有害语言和图像示例，建议读者谨慎阅读。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the under-explored safety risks associated with video-based large language models (Video LLMs), contrasting with the extensive studies on image-based counterparts. Previous methods have not adequately tackled the systemic risks posed by integrating video modalities, which have been shown to degrade safety performance significantly. The proposed approach, VideoSafety-R1, introduces a dual-stage framework that includes a novel VideoSafetyThinking dataset and employs Alarm Token-Guided Safety Fine-Tuning (AT-SFT) alongside safety-guided GRPO to enhance harm perception and defensive reasoning. The methodology demonstrates substantial improvements in safety performance, achieving a 71.1% increase on the VSE-HH benchmark and notable enhancements on several image safety datasets, thereby supporting the goal of advancing safety in Video LLMs.
本文探讨了视频大语言模型（Video LLMs）安全风险的不足研究，与对图像大语言模型的广泛研究形成对比。以往的方法未能充分应对视频模态整合所带来的系统性风险，这已被证明显著降低了安全性能。所提出的方法VideoSafety-R1引入了一个双阶段框架，通过创新组件如VideoSafetyThinking数据集、基于警报令牌的安全微调和安全引导的GRPO，有效地将安全对齐从单纯的伤害感知转向主动推理。该方法在VSE-HH任务上实现了71.1%的性能提升，并在多个图像安全数据集上取得显著进展，从而支持了提升视频大语言模型安全性的目标。
OffTopicEval: When Large Language Models Enter the Wrong Chat, Almost Always!
Authors: Jingdi Lei, Varun Gumma, Rishabh Bhardwaj, Seok Min Lim, Chuan Li, Amir Zadeh, Soujanya Poria
First: 2025-09-30T16:39:17+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-13T11:01:37+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large Language Model (LLM) safety is one of the most pressing challenges for enabling wide-scale deployment. While most studies and global discussions focus on generic harms, such as models assisting users in harming themselves or others, enterprises face a more fundamental concern: whether LLM-based agents are safe for their intended use case. To address this, we introduce operational safety, defined as an LLM's ability to appropriately accept or refuse user queries when tasked with a specific purpose. We further propose OffTopicEval, an evaluation suite and benchmark for measuring operational safety both in general and within specific agentic use cases. Our evaluations on six model families comprising 20 open-weight LLMs reveal that while performance varies across models, all of them remain highly operationally unsafe. Even the strongest models - Qwen-3 (235B) with 77.77% and Mistral (24B) with 79.96% - fall far short of reliable operational safety, while GPT models plateau in the 62-73% range, Phi achieves only mid-level scores (48-70%), and Gemma and Llama-3 collapse to 39.53% and 23.84%, respectively. While operational safety is a core model alignment issue, to suppress these failures, we propose prompt-based steering methods: query grounding (Q-ground) and system-prompt grounding (P-ground), which substantially improve OOD refusal. Q-ground provides consistent gains of up to 23%, while P-ground delivers even larger boosts, raising Llama-3.3 (70B) by 41% and Qwen-3 (30B) by 27%. These results highlight both the urgent need for operational safety interventions and the promise of prompt-based steering as a first step toward more reliable LLM-based agents.
中文标题/摘要
标题：OffTopicEval：当大型语言模型进入错误的聊天时，几乎总是如此！
大型语言模型（LLM）安全性是实现大规模部署的最紧迫挑战之一。虽然大多数研究和全球讨论集中在一般性危害上，例如模型帮助用户伤害自己或他人，但企业面临更根本的担忧：基于LLM的代理是否适合其预期用途。为了解决这个问题，我们引入了操作安全性，定义为LLM在特定目的下适当地接受或拒绝用户查询的能力。我们进一步提出了OffTopicEval，一个评估套件和基准，用于测量一般和特定代理使用案例中的操作安全性。我们对六个模型家族中20个开放权重LLM的评估显示，尽管模型之间的性能差异很大，但它们都高度不安全。即使是最强的模型——Qwen-3（235B）得分77.77%和Mistral（24B）得分79.96%——也远未达到可靠的操作安全性，而GPT模型的得分停留在62-73%范围内，Phi仅获得中等得分（48-70%），Gemma和Llama-3分别降至39.53%和23.84%。虽然操作安全性是核心模型对齐问题，为了抑制这些失败，我们提出了基于提示的引导方法：查询基础（Q-ground）和系统提示基础（P-ground），显著提高了OOD拒绝率。Q-ground提供了高达23%的持续增益，而P-ground则提供了更大的提升，将Llama-3.3（70B）提高了41%，将Qwen-3（30B）提高了27%。这些结果突显了对操作安全性干预的迫切需求，以及基于提示的引导作为朝着更可靠的基于LLM的代理迈出的第一步的前景。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical challenge of safety in Large Language Models (LLMs), particularly their operational safety in specific use cases, which has been largely overlooked in prior studies focused on generic harms. Existing methods have not adequately assessed whether LLMs can safely handle user queries for their intended purposes, leading to significant operational safety concerns. This paper introduces OffTopicEval, a novel evaluation suite designed to measure operational safety across various LLMs, revealing that all tested models exhibit high levels of operational unsafety, with performance metrics indicating that even the best models fall short of acceptable safety standards. The proposed methodology includes prompt-based steering techniques, specifically query grounding (Q-ground) and system-prompt grounding (P-ground), which significantly enhance out-of-distribution refusal rates, achieving performance improvements of up to 41% for certain models. These findings underscore the necessity for operational safety measures and demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed methods in advancing LLM reliability.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLM）在特定目的下处理用户查询的操作安全性问题。以往的方法主要集中在一般性危害上，忽视了企业在部署LLM时面临的特定安全问题。提出的OffTopicEval方法引入了一套评估工具，用于衡量不同模型家族的操作安全性，结果显示所有测试模型均存在显著的操作安全性不足。该方法包括基于提示的引导技术，特别是查询引导（Q-ground）和系统提示引导（P-ground），这些技术提高了对分布外（OOD）拒绝率的表现。实验结果表明，这些方法在操作安全性方面取得了显著改善，Q-ground的增益高达23%，而P-ground的提升更为显著，表明所提出的干预措施在解决识别出的安全问题方面的有效性。
Hiding in Plain Sight: A Steganographic Approach to Stealthy LLM Jailbreaks
Authors: Jianing Geng, Biao Yi, Zekun Fei, Ruiqi He, Lihai Nie, Tong Li, Zheli Liu
First: 2025-05-22T15:07:34+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-12T10:54:29+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Jailbreak attacks pose a serious threat to Large Language Models (LLMs) by bypassing their safety mechanisms. A truly advanced jailbreak is defined not only by its effectiveness but, more critically, by its stealthiness. However, existing methods face a fundamental trade-off between semantic stealth (hiding malicious intent) and linguistic stealth (appearing natural), leaving them vulnerable to detection. To resolve this trade-off, we propose StegoAttack, a framework that leverages steganography. The core insight is to embed a harmful query within a benign, semantically coherent paragraph. This design provides semantic stealth by concealing the existence of malicious content and ensures linguistic stealth by maintaining the natural fluency of the cover paragraph. We evaluate StegoAttack on four state-of-the-art, safety-aligned LLMs, including GPT-5 and Gemini-3, and benchmark it against eight leading jailbreak methods. Our results show that StegoAttack achieves an average attack success rate (ASR) of 95.50%, outperforming existing baselines across all four models. Critically, its ASR drops by less than 27.00% under external detectors, while maintaining natural language distribution. This demonstrates that steganography effectively decouples linguistic and semantic stealth, thereby posing a fully concealed yet highly effective security threat. The code is available at https://github.com/GenggengSvan/StegoAttack
中文标题/摘要
标题：明目张胆的隐匿：一种隐写学方法用于隐秘的LLM越狱
越狱攻击通过绕过大型语言模型（LLM）的安全机制，对其构成严重威胁。真正先进的越狱不仅以其有效性为特征，更重要的是其隐秘性。然而，现有方法在语义隐秘（隐藏恶意意图）和语言隐秘（看起来自然）之间面临根本的权衡，使其易于被检测。为了解决这一权衡，我们提出了StegoAttack，一个利用隐写学的框架。核心见解是将有害查询嵌入一个无害的、语义连贯的段落中。该设计通过隐藏恶意内容的存在提供语义隐秘，并通过保持覆盖段落的自然流畅性确保语言隐秘。我们在四个最先进的、安全对齐的LLM上评估StegoAttack，包括GPT-5和Gemini-3，并与八种领先的越狱方法进行基准测试。我们的结果表明，StegoAttack在所有四个模型上实现了95.50%的平均攻击成功率（ASR），超越了现有基线。关键是，在外部检测器下，其ASR下降不到27.00%，同时保持自然语言分布。这表明隐写学有效地解耦了语言和语义隐秘，从而构成了一个完全隐蔽但高度有效的安全威胁。代码可在https://github.com/GenggengSvan/StegoAttack获取。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the growing concern of jailbreak attacks on Large Language Models (LLMs), which exploit vulnerabilities in their safety mechanisms. Previous methods struggle with a trade-off between semantic stealth, which hides malicious intent, and linguistic stealth, which ensures natural language flow, making them susceptible to detection. The proposed StegoAttack framework utilizes steganography to embed harmful queries within benign, coherent paragraphs, effectively resolving this trade-off by concealing malicious content while maintaining fluency. This paper contributes a novel approach that significantly enhances the stealthiness of jailbreak attacks. The methodology involves evaluating StegoAttack against four advanced LLMs, including GPT-5 and Gemini-3, and comparing its performance with eight leading jailbreak methods, achieving an average attack success rate of 95.50% and demonstrating minimal performance degradation under external detection, thus supporting its goal of creating a highly effective yet concealed security threat.
本文探讨了针对大型语言模型（LLMs）的越狱攻击日益严重的威胁，这些攻击能够绕过安全机制并带来重大风险。以往的方法在语义隐蔽性（隐藏恶意意图）和语言隐蔽性（确保自然语言使用）之间存在权衡，使其易受检测。提出的StegoAttack框架利用隐写术将有害查询嵌入无害段落中，有效实现这两种隐蔽性。本文的贡献在于证明隐写术能够分离语言和语义隐蔽性，从而增强越狱攻击的有效性。该方法论涉及对StegoAttack在包括GPT-5和Gemini-3在内的四个先进LLM上的评估，并将其性能与八种现有越狱方法进行比较，平均攻击成功率达到95.50%，同时在外部检测下保持较低的检测率，从而支持其隐蔽性和有效性的目标。
ADVERSA: Measuring Multi-Turn Guardrail Degradation and Judge Reliability in Large Language Models
Authors: Harry Owiredu-Ashley
First: 2026-03-10T03:00:34+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-10T03:00:34+00:00
Comments: 12 pages, 12 figures. Independent research. Code and artifacts: https://github.com/Harry-Ashley/adversa-guardrail-degradation
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Most adversarial evaluations of large language model (LLM) safety assess single prompts and report binary pass/fail outcomes, which fails to capture how safety properties evolve under sustained adversarial interaction. We present ADVERSA, an automated red-teaming framework that measures guardrail degradation dynamics as continuous per-round compliance trajectories rather than discrete jailbreak events. ADVERSA uses a fine-tuned 70B attacker model (ADVERSA-Red, Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct with QLoRA) that eliminates the attacker-side safety refusals that render off-the-shelf models unreliable as attackers, scoring victim responses on a structured 5-point rubric that treats partial compliance as a distinct measurable state. We report a controlled experiment across three frontier victim models (Claude Opus 4.6, Gemini 3.1 Pro, GPT-5.2) using a triple-judge consensus architecture in which judge reliability is measured as a first-class research outcome rather than assumed. Across 15 conversations of up to 10 adversarial rounds, we observe a 26.7% jailbreak rate with an average jailbreak round of 1.25, suggesting that in this evaluation setting, successful jailbreaks were concentrated in early rounds rather than accumulating through sustained pressure. We document inter-judge agreement rates, self-judge scoring tendencies, attacker drift as a failure mode in fine-tuned attackers deployed out of their training distribution, and attacker refusals as a previously-underreported confound in victim resistance measurement. All limitations are stated explicitly. Attack prompts are withheld per responsible disclosure policy; all other experimental artifacts are released.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ADVERSA：测量大型语言模型中的多轮护栏退化和评审可靠性
大多数对大型语言模型（LLM）安全性的对抗性评估评估单个提示并报告二元通过/失败结果，这未能捕捉安全属性在持续对抗交互下的演变。我们提出了ADVERSA，一个自动化红队框架，测量护栏退化动态，作为连续的每轮合规轨迹，而不是离散的越狱事件。ADVERSA使用经过微调的70B攻击者模型（ADVERSA-Red，Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct与QLoRA），消除了攻击者侧的安全拒绝，使现成模型在攻击者中不可靠，使用结构化的5分制评分受害者响应，将部分合规视为一个独特的可测量状态。我们报告了在三个前沿受害者模型（Claude Opus 4.6，Gemini 3.1 Pro，GPT-5.2）上进行的受控实验，使用三重评审共识架构，其中评审可靠性被测量为一项一流的研究结果，而不是假设。在15次对话中，最多进行10轮对抗，我们观察到26.7%的越狱率，平均越狱轮次为1.25，这表明在该评估设置中，成功的越狱集中在早期轮次，而不是通过持续压力积累。我们记录了评审间一致性率、自我评审评分倾向、在超出其训练分布的微调攻击者中作为失败模式的攻击者漂移，以及作为受害者抵抗测量中先前未报告的混淆因素的攻击者拒绝。所有限制均明确说明。攻击提示根据负责任的披露政策被保留；所有其他实验材料均已发布。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of existing adversarial evaluations of large language models (LLMs), which typically assess safety through single prompts and binary outcomes, failing to capture the dynamics of safety properties during prolonged adversarial interactions. The proposed ADVERSA framework introduces a continuous measurement of guardrail degradation using a fine-tuned 70B attacker model, which improves reliability by eliminating safety refusals that affect off-the-shelf models. The study contributes by establishing judge reliability as a key research outcome and employing a triple-judge consensus architecture to evaluate three advanced victim models across multiple adversarial rounds. The methodology reveals a 26.7% jailbreak rate over 15 conversations, indicating that successful jailbreaks are more likely to occur in early rounds, while also documenting various failure modes and inter-judge agreement rates, thus providing a comprehensive understanding of model vulnerabilities and the reliability of evaluations.
本研究解决了现有大型语言模型（LLM）对抗性评估的局限性，这些评估通常通过单一提示和二元结果来评估安全性，未能捕捉在持续对抗交互中安全属性的动态变化。提出的ADVERSA框架引入了对护栏退化的连续测量，使用经过微调的70B攻击者模型，通过消除阻碍现成模型的安全拒绝来提高可靠性。该研究的贡献在于将评审者可靠性确立为关键结果，并采用三评审者共识架构评估三个先进的受害者模型，进行多轮对抗实验。该方法揭示了26.7%的越狱率，平均越狱轮次为1.25，表明脆弱性在初始交互中更为普遍，而不是随着时间的推移而累积，从而支持了对LLM安全评估进行持续评估的必要性。
Beyond Benchmarks: Dynamic, Automatic And Systematic Red-Teaming Agents For Trustworthy Medical Language Models
Authors: Jiazhen Pan, Bailiang Jian, Paul Hager, Yundi Zhang, Che Liu, Friedrike Jungmann, Hongwei Bran Li, Chenyu You, Junde Wu, Jiayuan Zhu, Fenglin Liu, Yuyuan Liu, Niklas Bubeck, Christian Wachinger, Chen, Chen, Zhenyu Gong, Cheng Ouyang, Georgios Kaissis, Benedikt Wiestler, Daniel Rueckert
First: 2025-07-30T08:44:22+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-09T15:37:01+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Ensuring the safety and reliability of large language models (LLMs) in clinical practice is critical to prevent patient harm. However, LLMs are advancing so rapidly that static benchmarks quickly become obsolete or prone to overfitting, yielding a misleading picture of model trustworthiness. Here we introduce a Dynamic, Automatic, and Systematic (DAS) red-teaming framework that continuously stress-tests LLMs across four safety-critical axes: robustness, privacy, bias/fairness, and hallucination. Validated against board-certified clinicians with high concordance, a suite of adversarial agents autonomously mutates clinical test cases to uncover vulnerabilities in real time. Applying DAS to 15 proprietary and open-source LLMs revealed a profound gap between high static benchmark performance and low dynamic reliability - the ``Benchmarking Gap''. Despite median MedQA accuracy exceeding 80\%, 94\% of previously correct answers failed our dynamic robustness tests. Crucially, this brittleness generalized to the realistic, open-ended HealthBench dataset, where top-tier models exhibited failure rates exceeding 70\% and stark shifts in model rankings across evaluations, suggesting that high scores on established static benchmarks may reflect superficial memorization. We observed similarly high failure rates across other domains: privacy leaks were elicited in 86\% of scenarios, cognitive-bias priming altered clinical recommendations in 81\% of fairness tests, and we identified hallucination rates exceeding 74\% in widely used models. By converting medical LLM safety analysis from a static checklist into a dynamic stress-test, DAS provides a foundational, scalable, and living platform to surface the latent risks that must be addressed before the next generation of medical AI can be safely deployed.
中文标题/摘要
标题：超越基准：动态、自动和系统化的红队代理用于可信的医疗语言模型
确保大型语言模型（LLMs）在临床实践中的安全性和可靠性对于防止患者伤害至关重要。然而，LLMs的发展速度如此之快，以至于静态基准很快变得过时或容易过拟合，从而产生误导性的模型可信度图景。在此，我们介绍了一种动态、自动和系统化（DAS）红队框架，持续对LLMs在四个安全关键轴上进行压力测试：鲁棒性、隐私、公平性/偏见和幻觉。经过与高一致性的董事会认证临床医生的验证，一套对抗性代理自主变更临床测试案例，以实时发现漏洞。将DAS应用于15个专有和开源LLMs揭示了高静态基准性能与低动态可靠性之间的深刻差距——“基准差距”。尽管中位数MedQA准确率超过80%，但94%的先前正确答案未能通过我们的动态鲁棒性测试。至关重要的是，这种脆弱性在现实的开放式HealthBench数据集中得到了普遍体现，顶级模型的失败率超过70%，并且在评估中模型排名发生了显著变化，这表明在既定静态基准上获得的高分可能反映了表面的记忆。我们在其他领域观察到了类似的高失败率：在86%的场景中引发了隐私泄露，81%的公平性测试中认知偏见的引导改变了临床建议，我们在广泛使用的模型中发现幻觉率超过74%。通过将医疗LLM安全分析从静态检查表转变为动态压力测试，DAS提供了一个基础的、可扩展的和动态的平台，以揭示必须在下一代医疗AI安全部署之前解决的潜在风险。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need for ensuring the safety and reliability of large language models (LLMs) in clinical practice, highlighting the limitations of static benchmarks that quickly become outdated and may misrepresent model trustworthiness. Previous methods relied on these static evaluations, which often led to misleading conclusions about model performance, whereas the proposed Dynamic, Automatic, and Systematic (DAS) red-teaming framework offers a continuous stress-testing approach across key safety dimensions such as robustness, privacy, bias/fairness, and hallucination. This paper contributes a novel methodology that autonomously mutates clinical test cases to identify vulnerabilities in real time, revealing a significant 'Benchmarking Gap' where high static performance does not correlate with dynamic reliability. The application of DAS to 15 LLMs demonstrated that despite a median MedQA accuracy exceeding 80%, 94% of previously correct answers failed dynamic robustness tests, with failure rates exceeding 70% on the HealthBench dataset, indicating that traditional benchmarks may reflect superficial memorization rather than true model reliability.
本研究关注确保大型语言模型（LLMs）在临床实践中的安全性和可靠性的重要性，强调了静态基准的局限性，这些基准很快就会过时，并可能误导对模型可信度的评估。以往的方法依赖于这些静态评估，常常导致对模型性能的误导性结论，而所提出的动态、自动和系统化（DAS）红队框架则提供了一种在关键安全维度（如鲁棒性、隐私、公平性/偏见和幻觉）上进行持续压力测试的方法。本文贡献了一种新颖的方法论，该方法能够实时自主变更临床测试案例，以识别脆弱性，揭示了一个显著的“基准差距”，即高静态性能与动态可靠性之间的脱节。对15个LLM应用DAS的结果表明，尽管MedQA的中位准确率超过80%，但94%的先前正确答案在动态鲁棒性测试中失败，HealthBench数据集上的失败率超过70%，这表明传统基准可能反映的是表面的记忆，而非真正的模型可靠性。
Automating Deception: Scalable Multi-Turn LLM Jailbreaks
Authors: Adarsh Kumarappan, Ananya Mujoo
First: 2025-11-24T03:15:11+00:00 · Latest: 2026-03-07T08:41:48+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Multi-turn conversational attacks, which leverage psychological principles like Foot-in-the-Door (FITD), where a small initial request paves the way for a more significant one, to bypass safety alignments, pose a persistent threat to Large Language Models (LLMs). Progress in defending against these attacks is hindered by a reliance on manual, hard-to-scale dataset creation. This paper introduces a novel, automated pipeline for generating large-scale, psychologically-grounded multi-turn jailbreak datasets. We systematically operationalize FITD techniques into reproducible templates, creating a benchmark of 1,500 scenarios across illegal activities and offensive content. We evaluate seven models from three major LLM families under both multi-turn (with history) and single-turn (without history) conditions. Our results reveal stark differences in contextual robustness: models in the GPT family demonstrate a significant vulnerability to conversational history, with Attack Success Rates (ASR) increasing by as much as 32 percentage points. In contrast, Google's Gemini 2.5 Flash exhibits exceptional resilience, proving nearly immune to these attacks, while Anthropic's Claude 3 Haiku shows strong but imperfect resistance. These findings highlight a critical divergence in how current safety architectures handle conversational context and underscore the need for defenses that can resist narrative-based manipulation.
中文标题/摘要
标题：自动化欺骗：可扩展的多轮LLM越狱
多轮对话攻击利用心理学原理，如“脚踏实地”（FITD），通过小的初始请求为更大的请求铺平道路，以绕过安全对齐，对大型语言模型（LLMs）构成持续威胁。防御这些攻击的进展受到依赖于手动、难以扩展的数据集创建的阻碍。本文介绍了一种新颖的自动化管道，用于生成大规模、基于心理学的多轮越狱数据集。我们系统地将FITD技术操作化为可重复的模板，创建了一个涵盖非法活动和攻击性内容的1,500个场景的基准。我们在多轮（有历史）和单轮（无历史）条件下评估了来自三个主要LLM家族的七个模型。我们的结果揭示了上下文鲁棒性方面的显著差异：GPT家族的模型对对话历史表现出显著脆弱性，攻击成功率（ASR）增加了多达32个百分点。相比之下，谷歌的Gemini 2.5 Flash表现出卓越的韧性，几乎对这些攻击免疫，而Anthropic的Claude 3 Haiku则显示出强但不完美的抵抗力。这些发现突显了当前安全架构在处理对话上下文方面的关键分歧，并强调了需要能够抵御叙事操控的防御措施。
Summary / 总结
This article addresses the ongoing threat posed by multi-turn conversational attacks on Large Language Models (LLMs), which exploit psychological principles such as the Foot-in-the-Door (FITD) technique to bypass safety measures. Previous methods for defending against these attacks have relied on manual dataset creation, which is difficult to scale and lacks efficiency. The proposed approach automates the generation of large-scale, psychologically-informed multi-turn jailbreak datasets, operationalizing FITD techniques into reproducible templates and creating a benchmark of 1,500 scenarios. The research methodology includes evaluating seven models from three major LLM families under both multi-turn and single-turn conditions, revealing significant differences in their contextual robustness. The findings indicate that while GPT models are highly vulnerable to conversational history, with Attack Success Rates increasing by up to 32 percentage points, Google's Gemini 2.5 Flash shows remarkable resilience, and Anthropic's Claude 3 Haiku demonstrates strong but imperfect resistance, highlighting the need for improved defenses against narrative-based manipulation.
本文探讨了多轮对话攻击对大型语言模型（LLMs）的日益关注，这些攻击利用心理学原理，如脚踏实地（FITD）技术，绕过安全措施。以往的方法依赖于手动数据集创建，这种方式不具可扩展性，限制了对这些攻击的防御效果。所提出的方法自动生成大规模、基于心理学的多轮越狱数据集，将FITD技术操作化为可重复的模板，并创建了1500个场景的基准。研究方法涉及在多轮和单轮条件下评估来自三个主要LLM家族的七个模型，揭示了它们在上下文鲁棒性方面的显著差异。研究结果表明，尽管GPT模型对对话历史特别脆弱，但谷歌的Gemini 2.5 Flash表现出显著的抗攻击能力，而Anthropic的Claude 3 Haiku则表现出强但不完美的抵抗力，强调了对抗叙事操控的防御需求。
